**Andhra Pradesh**[^cite_note-7] is a [state](375986.md) on the [east coast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_Coast_of_India) of [southern India](678583.md). The [seventh largest Indian state by area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_states_and_union_territories_of_India_by_area) and the [tenth largest by population](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_states_and_union_territories_of_India_by_population), it is bound by the [Bay of Bengal](47892.md) to the east, and shares borders with the states of [Odisha](250724.md) to the northeast, [Chhattisgarh](47734.md) to the north, [Telangana](990267.md) to northwest, [Karnataka](16880.md) to the west, and [Tamil Nadu](29918.md) to the south. [Telugu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Telugu_language), one of the [classical languages of India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Classical_languages_of_India), is the most widely spoken language in the state and serves as its official language. [Amaravati](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amaravati) is the state capital, and [Visakhapatnam](238760.md) is the largest city.&#x20;

Archaeological excavations have unearthed stone tools used by early [hominins](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hominini) and later [Neolithic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neolithic) settlements. The [Andhras](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andhras) are mentioned in the [Aitareya Brahmana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aitareya_Brahmana) (c. 800 [BCE](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BCE)) of the [Rigveda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rigveda). In the 4th century BCE, [Megasthenes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Megasthenes) described the Andhras living in the [Godavari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Godavari) and [Krishna River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Krishna_River) deltas, and the region later came under the control of the [Mauryan Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mauryan_Empire). The [Satavahana dynasty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Satavahana_dynasty) ruled over most of the Deccan from the 1st to 3rd century CE, with their capital at [Dhanyakataka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dharanikota). Subsequent major dynasties that ruled the region include the [Vishnukundinas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vishnukundina_dynasty), [Andhra Ikshvakus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andhra_Ikshvaku), [Pallavas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pallava_dynasty), [Eastern Chalukyas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Chalukyas), [Cholas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chola_dynasty), [Kakatiyas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kakatiya_dynasty), [Vijayanagara Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vijayanagara_Empire), and [Qutb Shahis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sultanate_of_Golconda). European colonisation began in the 17th century, with the British controlling much of the state as a part of the [Madras Presidency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madras_Presidency) for two centuries. After the Indian independence in 1947, [Andhra State](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andhra_State) was carved out of [Madras State](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madras_State) in 1953. In 1956, it merged with the Telugu-speaking regions of the former [Hyderabad State](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hyderabad_State_\(1948%E2%80%931956\)) to form [Andhra Pradesh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andhra_Pradesh_\(1956%E2%80%932014\)). A new state of [Telangana](990267.md) was bifurcated from it in 2014.&#x20;

The [Eastern Ghats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Ghats) separate the [coastal plains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Coastal_Plains) from the [Deccan Plateau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deccan_Plateau). Major rivers include Godavari, Krishna, [Tungabhadra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tungabhadra_River), and [Penna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Penna_River), which flow eastwards towards the Bay of Bengal. The state has significant reserves of [limestone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Limestone), [bauxite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bauxite), and a major proportion of India's [baryte](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baryte) reserves. As of September 2020, [agriculture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agriculture) and related activities employed 62.2% of the population, with [rice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rice) as the [staple crop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Staple_food). [Fisheries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fisheries) is a major contributor to the economy, with the state accounting for 30% of the country's fish production in 2022-23. The [Satish Dhawan Space Centre](2496664.md) in [Sriharikota](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sriharikota) serves as India's primary satellite launch centre.&#x20;

Andhra Pradesh has a diverse culture and is home to art forms like [Kuchipudi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kuchipudi), an [Indian classical dance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_classical_dance), and [Carnatic music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carnatic_music). It is the birthplace of the [Amaravati school of art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amaravati_art) that influenced South and Southeast Asian art. The state is home to a number of [pilgrimage centres](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tourism_in_Andhra_Pradesh#Religious_and_pilgrimage_sites) including the [Venkateswara temple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Venkateswara_Temple,_Tirumala) in [Tirumala](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tirumala) and [natural attractions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tourism_in_Andhra_Pradesh#Nature_tourism). Unique [geographical indications](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geographical_indication) from the state include [Bandar laddu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bandar_laddu), [Banganapalle mango](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Banganapalle_\(mango\)), [Dharmavaram silk sari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dharmavaram_silk_sari), [Kondapalli toys](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kondapalli_toys), and [Tirupati laddu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tirupati_laddu).&#x20;

## Etymology

Further information: [Andhras](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andhras)

See also: [List of Indian state and union territory name etymologies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Indian_state_and_union_territory_name_etymologies)

The *Andhras* are mentioned in the *[Aitareya Brahmana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aitareya_Brahmana)* of the *[Rigveda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rigveda)* (c. 800–500 BCE) as descendants of the sage [Vishvamitra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vishvamitra).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESen1999172-8][^cite_note-9] They are referred to as non-[Aryans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aryan) living on the fringes of Aryan settlements.[^cite_note-10][^cite_note-11] In [Puranic literature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Puranic_literature), they are referred to as *Andhras*, *Andhrara-jateeya*, and *[Andhrabhrtya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andhrabhrtya)*. According to scholars, the term *Andhra* functioned as both a tribal and a territorial name.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESen1999172-175-12] The region inhabited by the Andhras was called [Andhradesa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andhradesa), which was also known by other names including Andhramandala, Andhravisaya, Andhrabhumi, and Andhrapadha.[^cite_note-Desa-13] According to [Iravatham Mahadevan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iravatham_Mahadevan), the term *āndhra* is derived from the [Old Telugu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Old_Telugu) masculine nominative marker *aṉṟu*, possibly used by [Indo-Aryan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indo-Aryan_peoples) people or [Buddhists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buddhists) to refer to the [Telugu people](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Telugu_people).[^cite_note-14] [Pradesh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pradesh) means "state" or "province" in [Sanskrit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sanskrit) and several Indian languages.[^cite_note-15]&#x20;

## History

Main article: [History of Andhra Pradesh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_Andhra_Pradesh)

### Pre-history (before 4th century BCE)

Stone tools excavated from [Hanumanthunipadu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hanumanthunipadu) were dated to approximately 247,000 years old ([Middle Palaeolithic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Middle_Palaeolithic)). Although this finding suggests that archaic [Hominini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hominini) existed in the [Indian subcontinent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_subcontinent) long before the migration of modern humans from Africa began (about 125,000 years ago), the absence of fossil evidence has made it difficult to identify the people who made these tools.[^cite_note-Hominini-16] [Neolithic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neolithic) burial sites, and [petroglyphs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Petroglyph), dating back to 4,000 to 5,000 years ago were found in Achammakunta in [Palnadu district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palnadu_district) and Chakrala Bodu in [Markapuram district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Markapuram_district). These burial sites house extensive [cists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cist) and [dolmens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dolmen), aligned in the same direction and often surrounded by a wall.[^cite_note-Hominini-16][^cite_note-17]&#x20;

### Early history (4th century BCE to 4th century CE)



Ruins of the Buddhist Maha Stupa at [Bhattiprolu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bhattiprolu) (c. 3rd to 2nd century BCE)

Early [Hindu texts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hindu_texts) refer to the Andhras who lived in Andhradesa.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESen1999172-8][^cite_note-Desa-13] Greek historian [Megasthenes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Megasthenes) reported in his *[Indica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indica_\(Megasthenes\))* (c. 310 BCE) that Andhras lived in the [Godavari](47988.md) and [Krishna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Krishna) river deltas and were known for their military strength, which he ranked second only to the Mauryans in all [India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_subcontinent).[^cite_note-18][^cite_note-19][^cite_note-20] Archaeological evidence from sites such as [Amaravathi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amaravathi,_Guntur_district), [Bhattiprolu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bhattiprolu), and [Dharanikota](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dharanikota) suggests that the Andhra region might have been part of the [Maurya Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maurya_Empire) in the 3rd century BCE. After the death of Emperor [Ashoka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ashoka) in 232 BCE, the Maurya rule weakened and was replaced by several smaller kingdoms in the Andhra region.[^cite_note-21] [Buddhist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buddhist) relics from [Bhattiprolu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bhattiprolu) (c. 3rd to 2nd century BCE) bear [Brahmi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Br%C4%81hm%C4%AB_script) inscriptions, and the [Bhattiprolu script](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bhattiprolu_script) is considered a key for deciphering [Tamil Brahmi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamil_Brahmi).[^cite_note-22][^cite_note-23] The [Telugu script](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Telugu_script) evolved later from the [Kadamba script](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kadamba_script), which shares similarities with the Brahmi script.[^cite_note-24]&#x20;

The [Satavahana dynasty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Satavahana_dynasty) dominated the [Deccan Plateau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deccan_Plateau) from the 2nd century BCE to the 3rd century CE.[^cite_note-25] The Satavahanas ruled from their capital Dhanyakataka, which according to historian [Stanley Wolpert](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stanley_Wolpert), might have been the most prosperous city in India in the 2nd century.[^cite_note-26][^cite_note-27] [Roman coins](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_coins) unearthed from [Nagarjunakonda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nagarjunakonda) and the mention of the port of [Machilipatnam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Machilipatnam) (Maisolia) in the *[Periplus of the Erythraean Sea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Periplus_of_the_Erythraean_Sea)* (c. 1st century) indicate that trade relations existed with the [Roman Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_Empire) during the period.[^cite_note-28] Although uncertain, as per some scholars, [Nagarjuna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nagarjuna), the founder of the [Madhyamaka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madhyamaka) school of [Mahayana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mahayana), lived in the region during the Satavahana period.[^cite_note-29][^cite_note-30][^cite_note-31] The [Amaravati School of Art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amaravati_art), which is regarded as one of the three major styles of ancient Indian art, developed during the period and had a great influence on art in [South Asia](21566765.md) and [Southeast Asia](28741.md).[^cite_note-32]&#x20;

The [Andhra Ikshvakus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andhra_Ikshvaku), with their capital at [Vijayapuri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vijayapuri_South), succeeded the Satavahanas in the Krishna River valley in the 3rd century.[^cite_note-33] The [Salankayanas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salankayana_dynasty) ruled the region between Godavari and Krishna rivers with their capital at [Vengi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vengi) from the 4th to the early 5th century.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESen1999433-34]&#x20;

### Medieval history (5th to 16th century)



[Undavalli Caves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Undavalli_Caves), an example of the [rock-cut architecture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rock-cut_architecture) of the [Vishnukundinas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vishnukundina_dynasty) c. 5th to 6th century

The [Vishnukundinas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vishnukundina_dynasty) held sway over the region in the 5th and 6th centuries.[^cite_note-35] The [Undavalli Caves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Undavalli_Caves) are an example of [Indian rock-cut architecture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_rock-cut_architecture) of that time.[^cite_note-36] The [Telugu Cholas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Telugu_chodas) (Renati Chodas) ruled the south Andhra region from the 6th to the 13th centuries intermittently, initially as [samantas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samanta) of the [Pallavas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pallava_dynasty) and later under the [Cholas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chola_dynasty).[^cite_note-37][^cite_note-38] The oldest [Telugu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Telugu_language) inscription is the *Kalamalla sasanam*, dated to 575, which was carved during the reign of the Renati Choda king Erikal Muturaju Dhanunjaya.[^cite_note-39][^cite_note-40] The [Eastern Chalukyas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Chalukyas) ruled the [Vengi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vengi) region from the 7th century to early 12th century, and later continued to rule under the protection of the Cholas until 1189.[^cite_note-41] Early [Telugu literature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Telugu_literature) appeared during the Chalukya period.[^cite_note-42]&#x20;

The [Kakatiyas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kakatiya_dynasty) ruled the region between the late 12th and early 14th centuries.[^cite_note-Hari-43][^cite_note-44] After repeated attacks from the [Delhi Sultanate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi_Sultanate), the Kakatiyas fell in the early 14th century, which resulted in anarchy.[^cite_note-Hari-43] The [Musunuri Nayakas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Musunuri_Nayakas) briefly wrested power in northern Andhra,[^cite_note-Talbot-45] before the [Bahmani Kingdom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bahmani_Kingdom) took control of the region,[^cite_note-46][^cite_note-47] while the [Reddi Kingdom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reddi_Kingdom) ruled the coastal Andhra region.[^cite_note-Talbot-45] The Reddis constructed several fortifications including the [Kondaveedu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kondaveedu_Fort) and [Kondapalli Forts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kondapalli_Fort).[^cite_note-48][^cite_note-49]&#x20;

The [Gajapatis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gajapati_Kingdom) ruled parts of the region in the late 15th century before the region became part of the [Vijayanagara Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vijayanagara_Empire) during the reign of [Krishnadevaraya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Krishnadevaraya) in the early 16th century.[^cite_note-50] The [Pemmasani Nayaks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pemmasani_Nayaks) controlled parts of the northern Andhra region and were allied with the Vijayanagara Empire. The Nayaks had a large army that fought for the Vijayanagara kings against the Bahmani Kingdom.[^cite_note-51][^cite_note-52] During the Vijayanagara rule, several infrastructure projects were built including tanks and [anicuts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anicut).[^cite_note-WRD-53] The Vijayanagara kings also patronised art and literature, and built several temples and monuments.[^cite_note-54] The [Lepakshi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lepakshi) group of monuments, which are part of the tentative list of the UNESCO [World Heritage Sites](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Heritage_Sites), was built during the period.[^cite_note-55][^cite_note-56]&#x20;

### Later conflicts and European colonisation (17th to 20th century)



Map of India in 1765 (left) ad 1805 showing the regions ruled by the British [East India Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_India_Company), [Carnatic Sultanate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carnatic_Sultanate), [Kingdom of Mysore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingdom_of_Mysore), and [Nizam of Hyderabad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nizam_of_Hyderabad)

After the Vijayanagara Empire was defeated in the [Battle of Talikota](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Talikota) in 1565 by a confederacy of [Deccan sultanates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deccan_sultanates),[^cite_note-57] and the break-up of the sultanates,[^cite_note-58] the [Sultanate of Golconda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sultanate_of_Golconda) controlled parts of Andhra while the [Nayaks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nayak_caste) including Pemmasani Nayaks, [Sayapaneni Nayaks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sayapaneni_Nayaks), and [Nayakas of Kalahasti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nayakas_of_Kalahasti) supported the [Aravidu dynasty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aravidu_dynasty) and continued to ruled parts of the region.[^cite_note-59] Majority of the region later came under the rule of the [Mughal Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mughal_Empire) in the 17th century.[^cite_note-60] [Nizam-ul-Mulk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nizam-ul-Mulk,_Asaf_Jah_I), initially appointed as viceroy of the Deccan by the Mughals in 1713, established himself as a semi-independent ruler as the [Nizam of Hyderabad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nizam_of_Hyderabad).[^cite_note-61]&#x20;

A [French](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_India) factory was founded at Masulipatnam in 1669 after a grant from Golconda Sultanate, which was abandoned before being re-established after the French established a trading post at [Yanam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yanam) in 1723. The territories were ceded to the French by [Muzaffar Jang Hidayat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muzaffar_Jang_Hidayat), the 3rd Nizam of Hyderabad, in 1750.[^cite_note-Knight-62][^cite_note-Yanam-63] In 1765, [Robert Clive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_Clive) of the [East India Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_India_Company) obtained a grant of four circars (administrative districts) in the [Rayalaseema](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rayalaseema) region from the Mughal emperor [Shah Alam II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shah_Alam_II) and later formalised in a treaty with Nizam Ali, the 5th Nizam of Hyderabad, in 1778 to for an addition of another circar. The territories were later ceded to the British by Ali in 1800.[^cite_note-64] Meanwhile, in North Andhra, Raja Vijaya Ramaraju established the [Vizianagaram Estate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vizianagaram_Estate) with the help of the British support. However, after a dispute with the British, he was defeated at the [Battle of Padmanabham](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Padmanabham) in 1794 and the estate annexed as a tributary of the British.[^cite_note-65] Following the annexation of the [Carnatic Sultanate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carnatic_Sultanate) in 1801, the last major portion of present-day Andhra Pradesh came under British East India Company rule as part of [Madras Presidency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madras_Presidency).[^cite_note-66] The French territories changed hands multiple times between the British and the French, before they were returned to the French at the end of the [Napoleonic Wars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Napoleonic_Wars) in 1817. While Masulipatnam was later taken over by the British following several rounds of territory exchanges in the late 19th century, Yanam remained with the French.[^cite_note-Knight-62][^cite_note-Yanam-63]&#x20;



[Dowleswaram Barrage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dowleswaram_Barrage) built in 1850 by [Arthur Cotton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arthur_Cotton)

After the [Indian Rebellion of 1857](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Rebellion_of_1857), the region became part of the [British crown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_Raj).[^cite_note-67] During the British Raj, several irrigation facilities and waterways were developed, including the [Dowleswaram Barrage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dowleswaram_Barrage), built by [Arthur Cotton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arthur_Cotton).[^cite_note-WRD-53][^cite_note-68] The [Buckingham Canal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buckingham_Canal), built between 1806 and 1878, ran parallel to the [Coromandel Coast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coromandel_Coast) from [Kakinada](1684096.md) to [Marakkanam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marakkanam) and served as a major water transportation route.[^cite_note-69] [Telegraph](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Telegraph) service was initiated in 1850.[^cite_note-70] [Kandukuri Veeresalingam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kandukuri_Veeresalingam) pioneered the Telugu renaissance movement which promoted the education of women and lower-caste people, opposed [child marriage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Child_marriage), [dowry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dowry), and the prohibition of widow remarriage.[^cite_note-71] Armed uprisings against the British were led by [Uyyalawada Narasimha Reddy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Uyyalawada_Narasimha_Reddy) in the mid-19th century,[^cite_note-72] and [Kanneganti Hanumanthu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kanneganti_Hanumanthu) and [Alluri Sitarama Raju](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alluri_Sitarama_Raju) later mobilised farmers and [Adivasis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adivasi).[^cite_note-73][^cite_note-74] Other prominent people involved in the struggle for independence include [Tanguturi Prakasam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tanguturi_Prakasam) ("Andhra Kesari"),[^cite_note-75] [Gadicherla Harisarvottama Rao](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gadicherla_Harisarvottama_Rao),[^cite_note-76] and [Bhogaraju Pattabhi Sitaramayya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bhogaraju_Pattabhi_Sitaramayya).[^cite_note-77] [Pingali Venkayya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pingali_Venkayya), designed the initial tricolour flag, which was later adopted as the [Indian National Flag](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flag_of_India).[^cite_note-78][^cite_note-79] [Durgabai Deshmukh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Durgabai_Deshmukh) founded the [Andhra Mahila Sabha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andhra_Mahila_Sabha) in 1937.[^cite_note-80]&#x20;

### Post-Independence (1947–present)



[Potti Sreeramulu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Potti_Sreeramulu), whose fast unto death in 1952 led to the formation of [Andhra State](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andhra_State)

After the Independence of India in 1947, the Madras Presidency became [Madras State](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madras_State), comprising most of the present-day state of Andhra Pradesh. In November-December 1952, [Potti Sreeramulu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Potti_Sreeramulu) fasted to death at [Madras](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madras), demanding an independent state for the Telugu-speaking people of the Madras State.[^cite_note-81] On 1 October 1953, [Andhra state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andhra_state) was split from the Madras State, comprising of the Telugu-speaking areas, with [Kurnool](1039379.md) as its capital.[^cite_note-82] On 1 November 1956, the state was further reorganised to create [Andhra Pradesh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andhra_Pradesh_\(1956%E2%80%932014\)) when states were redrawn linguistically in [1956](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/States_Reorganisation_Act,_1956), with [Hyderabad](37534.md) as the capital.[^cite_note-83]&#x20;

From the 1950s, though the state experiences flucutations in growth, the region showed consistent economic and social development due to [reform-oriented](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economic_reforms_in_India) policies and social reforms.[^cite_note-84] In 2009, the [Government of India](553883.md) decided to bifurcate the state in response to the [Telangana movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Telangana_movement). The [Samaikyandhra Movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samaikyandhra_Movement), which protested the bifurcation, emerged in the Andhra region and led to political turmoil in the state.[^cite_note-85] The [Andhra Pradesh reorganisation act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andhra_Pradesh_Reorganisation_Act,_2014) was subsequently passed by the [Parliament of India](410238.md), authorising the formation of Telangana state, despite opposition from the state legislature.[^cite_note-86] The new state of Telangana came into existence on 2 June 2014 after approval from the [President of India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/President_of_India), with the residual state continuing as Andhra Pradesh.[^cite_note-87][^cite_note-88] While initially Hyderabad served as the capital for both the states, in 2017, the [Government of Andhra Pradesh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_of_Andhra_Pradesh) began operating from its new capital, [Amaravati](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amaravati).[^cite_note-89] A proposal to establish three capitals–Amaravati as the legislative capital, [Visakhapatnam](238760.md) as the executive capital, and Kurnool as the judicial capital, was proposed by the state government in 2020 and was struck down by the [Andhra Pradesh High Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andhra_Pradesh_High_Court).[^cite_note-90][^cite_note-91]&#x20;

## Environment

### Geography



Andhra Pradesh relief map

Main article: [Geography of Andhra Pradesh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geography_of_Andhra_Pradesh)

See also: [List of rivers in Andhra Pradesh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_rivers_in_Andhra_Pradesh)

Spread over 163,275 km<sup>2</sup> (63,041 sq mi), Andhra Pradesh is the [seventh-largest state by area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_states_and_union_territories_of_India_by_area) in India.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDOP20233-92] It shares borders with [Odisha](250724.md) to the northeast, [Chhattisgarh](47734.md) to the north, [Telangana](990267.md) to the northwest, [Karnataka](16880.md) to the southwest, [Tamil Nadu](29918.md) to the south, and the [Bay of Bengal](47892.md) to the east. The [enclave](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Enclave) of [Yanam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yanam_district), part of the [union territory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Union_territory) of [Puducherry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Puducherry_\(union_territory\)), is located along the eastern coast, bordering [Kakinada district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kakinada_district).[^cite_note-93] The state has a 974 km (605 mi) coastline, the second-longest amongst the Indian states.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDOP20233-92] The state comprises of two regions, namely [Coastal Andhra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coastal_Andhra) and [Rayalaseema](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rayalaseema).[^cite_note-94] The northern part of Coastal Andhra is sometimes referred to as [North Andhra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_Andhra).[^cite_note-95]&#x20;



[Tirumala](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tirumala) hills of the [Eastern Ghats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Ghats)

The [Eastern Ghats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Ghats), which run along the eastern coast, form a major dividing line between the coastal plains and the [peneplains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peneplain) along the edge of the [Deccan Plateau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deccan_Plateau) to the west. They form a discontinuous chain of mountains, dissected by several rivers and form several valleys,[^cite_note-Kohli-96] including the [Araku Valley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Araku_Valley),[^cite_note-97] and the [Gandikota](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gandikota) gorge in the Erramala hill range, through which the [Penna River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Penna_River) flows.[^cite_note-98] The various sections of the Eastern Ghats are known by various names such as [Nallamala Hills](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nallamala_Hills), [Seshachalam Hills](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seshachalam_Hills), [Papi Hills](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Papi_Hills), and [Horsley Hills](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Horsley_Hills). The hills are more pronounced toward the south and extreme north of the coast.[^cite_note-Kohli-96][^cite_note-99] [Arma Konda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arma_Konda), located in [Alluri Sitharama Raju district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alluri_Sitharama_Raju_district), is the highest peak in the state.[^cite_note-Kohli-96][^cite_note-100] The peneplains, which form part of the Rayalaseema region, gently slope eastwards.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDES2021107-101]&#x20;

The region between the Eastern Ghats and the Bay of Bengal form the [Eastern Coastal Plains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Coastal_Plains) of the coastal Andhra. The width of the coastal plains varies across regions, and are widest in the region of the deltas formed by the rivers [Godavari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Godavari) and [Krishna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Krishna_River).[^cite_note-UGC-102] The two river deltas form a contiguous single physiographic unit and cut across a 160 km (99 mi) wide gorge in the Eastern Ghats.[^cite_note-103] There are two large lakes, the [Kolleru Lake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kolleru_Lake), which have been pushed inwards due to these river deltas having advanced towards the Bay of Bengal, and the [Pulicat Lake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pulicat_Lake), which is interrupted by a sand spit which forms the island of [Sriharikota](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sriharikota).[^cite_note-UGC-102] There are several sandy beaches along the coast such as [Rushikonda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rushikonda), [Mypadu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mypadu), and [Suryalanka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suryalanka_Beach).[^cite_note-104]&#x20;

### Geology



[Karst](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karst) formations in [Belum Caves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Belum_Caves)

The state has five soil types, [Black](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chernozem), [alluvial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alluvial_soil), [clay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clay), [sand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sand), and [laterite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laterite_soil).[^cite_note-FG-105] The coastal plains are very fertile due to the aluvium deposited by the rivers and are used extensively for [agriculture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agriculture).[^cite_note-106][^cite_note-107][^cite_note-108]&#x20;

The Rayalaseema region has several cave formations, including the [Belum Caves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Belum_Caves), the country's second-longest cave system, and the [Borra Caves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Borra_Caves) are located in the Araku Valley.[^cite_note-109][^cite_note-110] These cave systems were formed by persistent hyrdological erosion over millions of years, and feature extensive [karst](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karst) formation.[^cite_note-111][^cite_note-112]&#x20;

The Kadapa basin, formed by two arching branches of the Eastern Ghats, is a mineral-rich area.[^cite_note-113] Major minerals found include [beach sand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beach_sand), [bauxite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bauxite), [limestone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Limestone), [granite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Granite), and [diamonds](8082.md), and minor minerals include [barytes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barytes), [calcite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Calcite), and [mica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mica).[^cite_note-114] The largest known [uranium](31743.md) reserves in the country are located in Tummalapalli in [Kadapa district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kadapa_district).[^cite_note-115] The state also has reserves of [oil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oil_reserves) and [natural gas](22131.md) reserves in the Godavari-Krishna basin.[^cite_note-PNG-116]&#x20;

### Climate



Climatic zones of India

The state has five sub-agro climatic zones– North Coastal, Southern, Krishna-Godavari delta, Scarce rainfall zone of Rayalaseema, and high altitude zones. The climate varies considerably across regions.[^cite_note-FG-105] Majority of the state experiences a predominantly [tropical climate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tropical_climate). The summer lasts from March to June, and are hot and humid. Maximum summer temperatures generally exceed 35 °C (95 °F) and often surpasses 40 °C (104 °F) in the central plateau region.[^cite_note-Climate-117] In the interiors, [heat waves](293270.md) are common during the summer, with temperatures sometimes reaching above 50 °C (122 °F).[^cite_note-118] The temperatures are lowest in the far southwest, with minimum still ranging above 20 °C (68 °F). Winter lasts from November to February with temperatures being slightly lower. The maximum temperature ranges from 30 to 35 °C (86 to 95 °F), except in the northeast, where the minimum may fall below 15 °C (59 °F).[^cite_note-Climate-117] [Lambasingi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lambasingi) generally records the lowest temperatures, ranging from 0 to 10 °C (32 to 50 °F).[^cite_note-119]&#x20;

The state gets an average annual rainfall of 966 mm (38.0 in),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDES202131-120] and majority of the rainfall from July to September during the [Southwest monsoon](57630.md#Southwest_monsoon).[^cite_note-Climate-117] The average rainfall widely varies from 500 to 1,500 mm (20 to 59 in) across regions. The arid zones in the west and southern areas of Rayalaseema receive the lowest rainfall.[^cite_note-FG-105] Low-pressure systems and [tropical cyclones](8282374.md) form in the Bay of Bengal during the [Northeast monsoon](57630.md#Northeast_monsoon) bring rains to the southern and coastal regions of the state from October to December.[^cite_note-121] The [North Indian Ocean tropical cyclones](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_Indian_Ocean_tropical_cyclone) occur throughout the year in the Bay of Bengal, bringing devastating winds and heavy rainfall.[^cite_note-122][^cite_note-123]&#x20;

### Flora and fauna

Further information: [Natural vegetation and wildlife of Andhra Pradesh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Natural_vegetation_and_wildlife_of_Andhra_Pradesh)



[Nagarjunsagar-Srisailam Tiger Reserve](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nagarjunsagar-Srisailam_Tiger_Reserve)

The state has a forest cover of 36,914.69 km<sup>2</sup> (14,252.84 sq mi), constituting 22.46% of the total land area.[^cite_note-Forest-124][^cite_note-125] The Eastern Ghats region contains dense [tropical forests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tropical_forest), while vegetation becomes sparser when transitioning to the peneplains, where dry [shrub](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shrub) vegetation is more common. The vegetation in the state is predominantly dry [deciduous](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deciduous) with trees like *[Tectona](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tectona)*, *[Terminalia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Terminalia_\(plant\))*, *[Dalbergia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dalbergia)*, and *[Pterocarpus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pterocarpus)*. The state has rare and endemic plants species such as *[Cycas beddomei](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cycas_beddomei)*, *[Pterocarpus santalinus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pterocarpus_santalinus)*, *[Terminalia pallida](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Terminalia_\(plant\))*, *[Syzygium alternifolium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syzygium_alternifolium)*, and *[Shorea tumburgia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shorea)*.[^cite_note-Forest-124][^cite_note-126] The state has 3,188.74 ha (7,879.5 acres) of [mangroves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mangrove) in the Krishna-Godavari river basin, the second largest such system in India.[^cite_note-Forest-124][^cite_note-Forest_AP-127]&#x20;

As of 2026, about 8,139.89 km<sup>2</sup> (3,142.83 sq mi) of the area has been notified as protected areas. The state has a [biosphere reserve](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Biosphere_reserve) at Seshachalam, three [national parks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_national_parks_of_India) and thirteen [wildlife sanctuaries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_wildlife_sanctuaries_of_India).[^cite_note-Forest-124][^cite_note-Forest_AP-127] The [Nagarjunsagar-Srisailam Tiger Reserve](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nagarjunsagar-Srisailam_Tiger_Reserve), covering an area of 3,728 km<sup>2</sup> (1,439 sq mi), forms part of the [Project Tiger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Project_Tiger), and is the largest [Tiger reserve](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tiger_reserves_of_India) in India.[^cite_note-Forest_AP-127][^cite_note-128] The [Koundinya Sanctuary and Rayala Elephant Reserve](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Koundinya_Wildlife_Sanctuary) forms part of the [Project Elephant](12609307.md) conservation programme.[^cite_note-Forest-124] The [Coringa Wildlife Sanctuary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coringa_Wildlife_Sanctuary) covers 582 km<sup>2</sup> (225 sq mi) of mangrove forests and salt-tolerant forest ecosystems near the sea.[^cite_note-129] Protected bird areas include [Kolleru Bird Sanctuary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kolleru_Bird_Sanctuary), a [Ramsar site](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ramsar_site) and [Nelapattu Bird Sanctuary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nelapattu_Bird_Sanctuary).[^cite_note-130][^cite_note-131]&#x20;



[Rose-ringed parakeet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rose-ringed_parakeet) feeding on [neem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azadirachta_indica)

The fauna found in the state include [Bengal tiger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bengal_tiger), [Asian elephant](379035.md), [gaur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaur), [leopard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leopard), [sloth bear](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sloth_bear), [sambar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sambar_\(deer\)), [chital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chital), [mouse-deer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mouse-deer), [barking deer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barking_deer), [wild boar](52255.md), [wild dog](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wild_dog), and [four-horned antelope](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Four-horned_antelope). Smallar [mammals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mammals) include [common langur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Common_langur), [bonnet macaque](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bonnet_macaque), [Indian hare](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_hare), [Indian porcupine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_porcupine), [Indian giant squirrel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_giant_squirrel), and [Indian pangolin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_pangolin). [Endangered](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Endangered) and [endemic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Endemism) species include [Jerdon's courser](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jerdon%27s_courser), [golden gecko](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golden_gecko), and [slender loris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Slender_loris). The estuaries of the Godavari and Krishna rivers support mangrove forests, with [fishing cats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fishing_cat) and [otters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Otter) as [keystone species](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Keystone_species).[^cite_note-Forest-124][^cite_note-Forest_AP-127]&#x20;

The bird species found in the state include [Asian open-billed stork](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asian_open-billed_stork), [black ibis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black_ibis), [black kite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black_kite), [black-shouldered kite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black-shouldered_kite), [cattle egret](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cattle_egret), [common pochard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Common_pochard), [common teal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Common_teal), [darter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Darter), [gadwall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gadwall), [great cormorant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_cormorant), [great Indian bustard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Indian_bustard), [grey heron](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grey_heron), [Indian peafowl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_peafowl), [Indian pond heron](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_pond_heron), [lesser florican](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lesser_florican), [lesser whistling duck](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lesser_whistling_duck), [little cormorant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Little_cormorant), [northern pintail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_pintail), [painted stork](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Painted_stork), [purple heron](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Purple_heron), [red-crested pochard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red-crested_pochard), [spot-billed duck](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spot-billed_duck), and [white ibis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_ibis). [Reptile](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reptile) species include [bamboo pit viper](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bamboo_pit_viper), [Leschenault's leaf-toed gecko](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leschenault%27s_leaf-toed_gecko), [brown vine snake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brown_vine_snake), [checkered keelback](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Checkered_keelback), [common garden lizard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Common_garden_lizard), [common krait](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Common_krait), [common skink](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Common_skink), [forest calotes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Calotes_calotes), [golden gecko](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golden_gecko), [green vine snake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Green_vine_snake), [house gecko](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/House_gecko), [Indian chameleon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_chameleon), [Indian cobra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_cobra), [Bengal monitor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bengal_monitor), [Indian python](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_python), [marsh crocodile](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marsh_crocodile), [rainbow water snake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rainbow_water_snake), [red-crested pochard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red-crested_pochard), [Russell's viper](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russell%27s_viper), [saw-scaled viper](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saw-scaled_viper), [Indian star tortoise](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_star_tortoise), and [yellow-collared wolf snake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yellow-collared_wolf_snake).[^cite_note-Forest-124][^cite_note-Forest_AP-127]&#x20;

| Animal | Bird | Tree | Fruit | Flower |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| [Blackbuck](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blackbuck) (*Antilope cervicapra*) | [Rose-ringed parakeet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rose-ringed_parakeet) (*Psittacula krameri*) | [Neem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azadirachta_indica) (*Azadirachta indica*) | [Mango](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mango) (*Mangifera indica*) | [Jasmine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jasmine) (*Jasminum officinale*) |

## Demographics

Further information: [Demographics of Andhra Pradesh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demographics_of_Andhra_Pradesh)

According to the [2011 Census of India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_Census_of_India), Andhra Pradesh had a population of 49,577,103 with a density of 304/km<sup>2</sup> (790/sq mi). The rural population accounted for 70.53% and the urban population accounted for the remaining 29.47%.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDOP20233-92] About 10.6% of the population were below the age of six years.[^cite_note-132] [Scheduled Castes and Scheduled Tribes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scheduled_Castes_and_Scheduled_Tribes) constituted 22.61% of the population.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDOP202316-133] The state had a [sex ratio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_sex_ratio) of 997 females per 1000 males, higher than the national average of 926 per 1000. The [literacy rate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Literacy) was 67.35%, with [West Godavari district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Godavari_district) recording the highest literacy rate of 74.32%, and the [Vizianagaram district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vizianagaram_district) having the lowest literary rate of 58.89%.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDOP20233-92] As of 2022, the state had a [Human Development Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_Development_Index) of 0.673.[^cite_note-134] As of 1 January 2023, there were 39,984,868 registered voters, including 3,924 [third gender](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Third_gender) voters. [Kurnool district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kurnool_district) had the maximum number of voters at 1,942,233, while [Alluri Sitharama Raju district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alluri_Sitharama_Raju_district) had the lowest registered voters at 729,085.[^cite_note-135]&#x20;

### Language

| Language |  | Percent |
| --- | --- | --- |
| [Telugu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Telugu_language) |  | 89.21% |
| [Urdu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urdu) |  | 6.55% |
| [Tamil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamil_language) |  | 1.04% |
| Other or not stated |  | 3.2% |

Telugu is the official language of the state, with [Urdu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urdu) designated as the second official language.[^cite_note-138] Telugu is the most spoken language and serves as the mother tongue of 89.21% of the population, followed by Urdu (6.55%) and [Tamil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamil_language) (1.04%).[^cite_note-lang1-136][^cite_note-Lang-137] [Kannada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kannada) and [Odia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Odia_language) are spoken by a minor population, and [Lambadi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lambadi) and other languages are spoken by the tribal people.[^cite_note-139] According to the Indian readership survey in 2019, 19% of the population aged 12 years and older could read and understand [English](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/English_language).[^cite_note-140]&#x20;

### Religion and ethnicity

Further information: [Religion in Andhra Pradesh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Religion_in_Andhra_Pradesh) and [Ethnic groups of Andhra Pradesh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ethnic_groups_of_Andhra_Pradesh)

According to the 2011 census, Hindus formed the majority with 90.89% of the population adhering to the religion, followed by [Muslims](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islam_in_Andhra_Pradesh) (7.30%), and [Christians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christianity_in_Andhra_Pradesh) (1.38%).[^cite_note-141][^cite_note-142] Popular Hindu pilgrimage destinations include the [Venkateswara temple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Venkateswara_Temple,_Tirumala) at [Tirumala](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tirumala), the [Mallikarjuna temple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mallikarjuna_Temple,_Srisailam) at [Srisailam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Srisailam), [Kanaka Durga Temple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kanaka_Durga_Temple) at [Vijayawada](750441.md), and the [Varaha Lakshmi Narasimha temple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Varaha_Lakshmi_Narasimha_temple,_Simhachalam) at [Simhachalam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Simhachalam).[^cite_note-143] [Buddhist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buddhist) religious sites include [Amaravati](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amaravathi,_Palnadu_district) and [Nagarjuna Konda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nagarjunakonda).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAPTDC201918–25-144]&#x20;

There were 104 castes which were classified as [Other Backward Classes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Other_Backward_Class), 59 Scheduled Castes and 34 Scheduled Tribes.[^cite_note-145][^cite_note-146][^cite_note-147] [Brahmin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Telugu_Brahmin), [Kamma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kamma_\(caste\)), [Kapu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kapu_\(caste\)), [Komati](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Komati_\(caste\)), [Raju](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Raju), [Reddy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reddy), and [Velama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Velama) communities are classified as [forward castes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forward_caste) in the state.[^cite_note-148][^cite_note-149]&#x20;

### Cities and towns

As per the 2011 census, the state had an urban population of 14.9 million.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDOP202318-150] [Visakhapatnam](238760.md) and [Vijayawada](750441.md) are the largest cities, and are the only urban agglomerations with [more than a million](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_million-plus_urban_agglomerations_in_India) inhabitants.[^cite_note-Urban-151]&#x20;
