## Culture

Main article: [Culture of Andhra Pradesh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Culture_of_Andhra_Pradesh)

### Clothing



[Kalamkari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kalamkari) artwork on a cloth

The men's traditional wear consists of *[panche](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dhoti)*, a 4.5 m (15 ft) long, rectangular piece of non-stitched white cloth often bordered in brightly coloured stripes.[^cite_note-153] Women traditionally wear a *[sari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sari)*, a garment that consists of a drape varying from 5 to 9 yd (4.6 to 8.2 m) in length and 2 to 4 ft (0.61 to 1.22 m) in breadth. It is typically wrapped around the waist, with one end draped over the shoulder, baring the midriff, as according to [Indian philosophy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_philosophy), the navel is considered as the source of life and creativity.[^cite_note-154][^cite_note-155] Women wear colourful [silk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Silk) saris on special occasions such as marriages.[^cite_note-156] The traditional wear of young girls consists of a long skirt with an over cloth (half-sari) worn over a [blouse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blouse).[^cite_note-157] People in urban areas generally wear tailored clothing, and western dress is popular. Western-style school uniforms are worn by both boys and girls in schools, even in rural areas.[^cite_note-158]&#x20;

[Kalamkari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kalamkari) is a textile printing art, that originated in the state. It uses [natural dyes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Natural_dyes), and the hand painting technique is used in [Srikalahasti Kalamkari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Srikalahasti_Kalamkari), while the [Pedana Kalamkari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pedana_Kalamkari) uses block painting.[^cite_note-159][^cite_note-160] [Dharmavaram silk saris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dharmavaram_silk_saris), Pedana, and Srikalahasti Kalamkari handicrafts are recognised as geographical indications.[^cite_note-GIlist-161]&#x20;

### Cuisine

Further information: [Telugu cuisine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Telugu_cuisine)



Full-course vegetarian Andhra meal

The Andhra cuisine incorporates spicy, tangy, and sweet flavours. [Chillies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chili_pepper), [curry leaves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Curry_leaves), [tamarind](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamarind), [gongura](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gongura) leaves, and [spices](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spices) are commonly used ingredients.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAPTDC201954–63-162][^cite_note-Cuisine-163] [Rice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rice) is the staple food, and is served with other accompaniments during a meal. These include *pappu* is a type of [dal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dal)-based food item that, made using lentils in combination with vegetables such as [tomato](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tomato), [spinach](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spinach), gongura, or [ridge gourd](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ridge_gourd), and *[charu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rasam_\(dish\))*, a type of watery soup made with tamarind juice and spices. It is served with traditional [curries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Curry), and a *pulusu*, a stew made using tamarind juice in combination with vegetables or meat. Other accompaniments include *pachadi*, a paste usually made with a combination of [groundnuts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peanut), fried vegetables, and chillies, and [pickles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Asian_pickle) made using raw mangoes, [gooseberries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gooseberry), or [lemons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lemon). [Buttermilk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buttermilk) or [curd](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Curd) is eaten towards the end of the meal to soothe the body, especially after eating spicy food items earlier.[^cite_note-Cuisine-163]&#x20;

*Pulihora* is rice based dish made with from a mixture of thick sauce of tamarind, chillies, and [asafoetida](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asafoetida), and tempered with [mustard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mustard_\(condiment\)), curry leaves, and peanuts. It is often offered to Hindu gods and then served as a *[prasadam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prasada)* (divine offering) in the temples. Popular vegetarian curries include *dondakaya koora* ([ivy gourd](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ivy_gourd) cooked with [coconut](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coconut) and green chilli) and *guttivankaya koora*(stuffed [egg plant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Egg_plant) curry). *Thalakaya koora* (lamb head curry), *royyala koora* (prawns curry), and *Natukodi koora* (chicken curry) are popular non-vegetarian dishes in the coastal regions. [Biryani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Biryani) types popular in Rayalaseema region include *yeta mamsam kobbari* (mutton), *seema kodi* (chicken), *Chennuru dum biryani*, and *Gongura mamsam* (mutton with gongura). *Pulasa pulusu* is a thick aromatic and tangy gravy made from the Godavari pulasa fish in [Konaseema](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Konaseema_district).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAPTDC201960-62-164]&#x20;

*[Ariselu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ariselu)*, *burelu*, *[laddu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laddu)*, and *[pootharekulu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pootharekulu)* are some of the sweets made for special festivals and occasions. *Pootharekulu* is made from sugar and rice flour. Other sweets include *taandri*, which is a mango-flavoured sun-dried fruit jelly and *khaaja*, a multi layered, flour-based, deep fried sweet dipped in [sugar syrup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sugar_syrup).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAPTDC201960-62-164] [Bandar laddu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bandar_laddu) and [Tirupati laddu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tirupati_laddu) have been accorded geographical indication status.[^cite_note-165][^cite_note-GIlist-161]&#x20;

### Literature

Further information: [Telugu literature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Telugu_literature)

The earliest Telugu writings come from inscriptions dates to the early [Middle Ages](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Middle_Ages). Inscriptions dated to 6th to 8th century indicate the evolution of Telugu as a separate language, with inscriptions from 8th to 10th centuries depict a more evolved form of the language used in poems and verses.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESithapathi19683-166] The 6th or 7th century Sanskrit text *[Janashrayi-Chhandovichiti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Janashrayi-Chhandovichiti)* deals with the [metres](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metre_\(poetry\)) used in Telugu, including some metres that are not found in [Sanskrit prosody](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sanskrit_prosody).[^cite_note-167]&#x20;



[Kandukuri Veeresalingam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kandukuri_Veeresalingam), the father of the Telugu renaissance movement

[Nannayya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nannayya), who lived in the 11th century wrote the first treatise on Telugu grammar, called *Andhra Shabda Chintamani* in Sanskrit.[^cite_note-168] He started translating the [Hindu epic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hindu_epic) of *[Mahabharata](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mahabharata)*.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESithapathi19685-169] The translation was continued by [Tikkana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tikkana) in the 13th century, and was completed by [Yerrapragada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yerrapragada) in the 14th century.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPandippediMantripragada192842–49-170][^cite_note-FOOTNOTESithapathi19685-6-171] There was a rise in several [Shaivism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shaivism) related texts during the 12th and 13th centuries. Most prominent from the period include [Mallikarjuna Panditaradhya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mallikarjuna_Panditaradhya), the author of *Sivatatva Saram*, [Palkuriki Somanatha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palkuriki_Somanatha), the author of *[Basava Purana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Basava_Purana)* and other works, Yadavakula Annamayya, the author of *Sarveswara Salakam*, and [Nanne Choda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nanne_Choda), who wrote *[Kumarasambhavam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kumarasambhavam)*.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESithapathi19689-12-172] The *[Ramayana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ramayana)* became popular in the 13th century, and the earliest Telugu translation was the *[Ranganatha Ramayanam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ranganatha_Ramayanam)* by [Gona Budda Reddy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gona_Budda_Reddy) in 1250.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESithapathi196813-14-173] [Pothana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pothana) translated *[Bhagavatam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bhagavata_Purana)* into Telugu in the 15th century.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPandippediMantripragada192864-174] The [Prabandha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prabandha) genre of Telugu literature flourished during the 16th century. The *[Ashtadiggajas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ashtadiggajas)* were the eight prominent scholars and poets in the court of Krishnadevaraya. These included [Allasani Peddana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Allasani_Peddana), [Nandi Thimmana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nandi_Thimmana), [Madayyagari Mallana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madayyagari_Mallana), [Dhurjati](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dhurjati), [Ayyalaraju Ramambhadrudu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ayyalaraju_Ramambhadrudu), [Pingali Surana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pingali_Surana), [Ramarajabhushanudu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ramarajabhushanudu), and [Tenali Ramakrishna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tenali_Ramakrishna).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESithapathi196826-38-175] [Vemana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vemana) was an Indian philosopher, who wrote Telugu poems using simple language and native idioms on a variety of subjects including [yoga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yoga), wisdom, and morality in the 17th century.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPandippediMantripragada192899-176] [Potuluri Veerabrahmam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Potuluri_Veerabrahmam), a clairvoyant and social reformer of 17th century, wrote *[Kalagnanam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kalagnanam)*, a book of predictions.[^cite_note-177][^cite_note-178]&#x20;

Veeresalingam, who pioneered the Telugu renaissance movement, brought about changes to the Telugu literature and education in the late 19th century. Telugu literature after the period is termed *Adhunika Telugu Sahityam* (modern Telugu literature). He was the author of the first Telugu [social novel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social_novel) *Rajasekhara Charitram*, published in 1880.[^cite_note-179] Other contemporary poets from this period include [Korada Ramachandra Sastri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Korada_Ramachandra_Sastri) and [Gurazada Srirama Murty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gurazada_Srirama_Murty).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESithapathi1968114-116-180] During the period, [Charles Philip Brown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charles_Philip_Brown) conducted pioneering work in bringing Telugu into the print era.[^cite_note-181] The use of colloquial idiom rather than *grandhik* (classical) in literature was championed by [Gurajada Apparao](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gurajada_Apparao) and [Gidugu Venkata Ramamurthy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gidugu_Venkata_Ramamurthy).[^cite_note-182] Various forms of literature such as poetry, novel, short story evolved in response to social developments during the period.[^cite_note-Dasari-183] [Rayaprolu Subba Rao](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rayaprolu_Subba_Rao), [Gurram Jashuva](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gurram_Jashuva), [Viswanatha Satyanarayana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Viswanatha_Satyanarayana), [Devulapalli Krishnasastri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Devulapalli_Krishnasastri), and [Sri Sri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sri_Sri_\(writer\)) were some of the Telugu poets from the period.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESithapathi1968159-173-184] [Palagummi Padmaraju](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palagummi_Padmaraju)'s short story *Galivaana* won second prize in World Short Story competition in 1952.[^cite_note-185] [Rachakonda Viswanatha Sastry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rachakonda_Viswanatha_Sastry) initiated a new trend by focussing on the downtrodden in his novel Raju-Mahishi. Women writers such as [Malathi Chendur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malathi_Chendur) and [Ranganayakamma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ranganayakamma) wrote several novels in the 1950s and 1960s. In the late 1970s, [Yandamuri Veerendranath](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yandamuri_Veerendranath) started writing popular novels which were serialised in magazines.[^cite_note-Dasari-183] [Viswanatha Satyanarayana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Viswanatha_Satyanarayana) was conferred the first [Jnanpith Award](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jnanpith_Award) for Telugu literature in 1970.[^cite_note-186]&#x20;

### Architecture



[Venkateswara Temple, Tirumala](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Venkateswara_Temple,_Tirumala), with the *[gopuram](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gopuram)* in the foreground and the gold-plated *[vimana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vimana)* in the background

Traditional temple architecture in the state is influenced by [Dravidian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dravidian_architecture) and [Vijayanagara architecture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vijayanagara_architecture). In Dravidian architecture, the temples consist of porches or *mantapas* preceding the door leading to the sanctum, gate-pyramids or *[gopurams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gopuram)* in quadrangular enclosures that surround the temple, and pillared halls. Besides these, the temple usually has a [tank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Temple_tank) called the *kalyani* or *pushkarni*.[^cite_note-187] The gopuram is a monumental tower, usually ornate at the entrance of the temple and forms a prominent feature.[^cite_note-188] They are topped by the *kalasam*, a bulbous stone [finial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Finial) and function as gateways through the walls that surround the temple complex.[^cite_note-189] *[Vimanam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vimana_\(architectural_feature\))* are similar structures built over the *[garbhagriha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Garbhagriha)* or inner sanctum of the temple but are usually smaller than the gopurams.[^cite_note-190] In the Vijayanagar style, the temple consists of three parts, the assembly hall known as the *mukha* [mantapa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mantapa), *arda* mantapa or *antarala* (ante chamber), and the *garbhagriha* or the sanctum sanctorum.[^cite_note-191] The civic architecture which featured round houses with elaborate courtyards is giving way to smaller apartments in the 21st century.[^cite_note-192]&#x20;

### Arts



[Kondapalli Toys](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kondapalli_Toys)

Further information: [Music of Andhra Pradesh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Music_of_Andhra_Pradesh) and [Telugu theatre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Telugu_theatre)

[Kuchipudi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kuchipudi) is an [Indian classical dance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_classical_dance) form that originated in the village of [Kuchipudi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kuchipudi,_Krishna_district) in Krishna district.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDevi199067-193] [Andhra Natyam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andhra_Natyam) is another traditional dance form that originated from Hindu temple dance.[^cite_note-194] [Kolannalu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kolattam) is a folk dance form usually performed by women using two short wooden sticks, one in each hand.[^cite_note-195][^cite_note-196] [Tholu bommalata](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tholu_bommalata) is a [shadow puppet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shadow_puppet) theatre tradition of the state.[^cite_note-197] [Kondapalli Toys](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kondapalli_Toys), the soft [limestone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Limestone) idol carvings from [Durgi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Durgi,_Palnadu_district),[^cite_note-198] and [Etikoppaka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Etikoppaka) lacquered wooden toys are unique traditional arts that have been recognised as geographical indications.[^cite_note-GIlist-161][^cite_note-199]&#x20;

*[Harikatha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harikatha)* involves the narration of Hindu mythological stories along with songs.[^cite_note-200] *[Burra katha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Burra_katha)* is an [oral storytelling](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oral_storytelling) art form in which Hindu mythological stories or contemporary social issues are narrated with music.[^cite_note-201] *Veethi Bhagavathamu* is another form of street theatre.[^cite_note-202] Drama is a popular [theatre art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Theatre_of_India) performed with folk stories or themes based on contemporary issues.[^cite_note-203] *[Kanyasulkam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kanyasulkam)*, written by Gurajada Apparao and presented in 1892, was the first Telugu drama to portray social issues.[^cite_note-204][^cite_note-205] Composers of [Carnatic music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carnatic_music) like [Annamacharya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Annamacharya), [Kshetrayya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kshetrayya), [Tyagaraja](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tyagaraja), and [Bhadrachala Ramadasu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bhadrachala_Ramadasu) hailed from the region.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDevi199075-206][^cite_note-207] *Sannai* and *Dolu* are common traditional musical instruments played during functions.[^cite_note-208]&#x20;



[Bapu Museum, Vijayawada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bapu_Museum,_Vijayawada)

There are 32 museums in the state featuring various collections.[^cite_note-Seth-209] The [Amaravathi archaeological museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Archaeological_Museum,_Amaravati) displays art traditions of Amaravathi and images of Buddha.[^cite_note-210] The [Bapu Museum, Vijayawada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bapu_Museum,_Vijayawada) houses historical artefacts.[^cite_note-211] [Telugu Samskruthika Niketanam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Telugu_Samskruthika_Niketanam) in Visakhapatnam displays artefacts related to Telugu culture and history.[^cite_note-Seth-209]<sup>: 19 </sup> The [Archaeological Survey of India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Archaeological_Survey_of_India) manages 135 protected monuments in the state.[^cite_note-212]&#x20;

Though the Telugu film industry, known as "[Tollywood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Telugu_cinema)", is primarily based in Hyderabad, several films are shot across the state.[^cite_note-213] Efforts have been made in the 2020s to encourage the Telugu film industry in the state.[^cite_note-214] [C. Pullaiah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/C._Pullaiah) is cited as the father of the Telugu cinema.[^cite_note-215][^cite_note-216] Film producer [D. Ramanaidu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/D._Ramanaidu) holds a [Guinness record](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guinness_World_Records) for the most films produced by a person.[^cite_note-217] Popular playback singers from the state include [S. P. Balasubrahmanyam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S._P._Balasubrahmanyam),[^cite_note-218] [P. Susheela](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/P._Susheela), and [S. Janaki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S._Janaki).[^cite_note-219]&#x20;

### Festivals



People decorate thir homes with colourful geometric designs called muggulu

*[Sankranti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Makar_Sankranti)* is the major harvest festival celebrated across the state. It is dedicated to the [Sun](26751.md) deity [Surya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Surya) and marks the end of [winter solstice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Winter_solstice).[^cite_note-220][^cite_note-221] It is traditionally a four-day festival, and people heir homes with colourful geometric designs called muggulu made from rice powder.[^cite_note-222]&#x20;

The first day of the Telugu new year is celebrated as *[Ugadi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ugadi)* which occurs during March/April on the [Gregorian calendar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gregorian_calendar). On the day, people prepare and share pickles (*pachhadi*) made from raw mangoes, neem flowers, pepper powder, jaggery, and tamarind. The pickle which is a mix of different tastes is meant to represent the difference experiences in life. Celebrations end with the recitation of the astrological predictions for the incoming year known as *Panchanga sravanam*.[^cite_note-Festivals-223]&#x20;

Other major [Hindu festivals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hindu_festivals) celebrated in the state include [Vijayadashami](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vijayadashami), [Deepavali](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diwali), [Ratha Saptami](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ratha_Saptami), [Krishna Janmashtami](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Krishna_Janmashtami), [Vinayaka Chaturthi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vinayaka_Chaturthi), [Mahashivratri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mahashivratri), and [Vaikunta Ekadasi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vaikunta_Ekadasi).[^cite_note-Festivals-223] Islamic festivals include [Eid](154944.md),[^cite_note-224] and [Rottela Panduga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rottela_Panduga) celebrated at [Bara Shaheed Dargah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bara_Shaheed_Dargah).[^cite_note-225] Christians festivals include [Good Friday](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Good_Friday),[^cite_note-226] [Easter](9325.md),[^cite_note-227] and [Christmas](6237.md).[^cite_note-228]&#x20;

## Administration and politics

### Administration



Districts of Andhra Pradesh

Amravati is the capital of the state and houses the state executive, legislative and head of judiciary.[^cite_note-229] There are 41 departments of the state and the departments have further sub-divisions which may govern various undertakings and boards.[^cite_note-230] The state is divided into 28 [districts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_districts_of_Andhra_Pradesh), each of which is administered by a [District Collector](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/District_Collector).[^cite_note-231][^cite_note-Data-232] For land administration, these are further divided into 82 [revenue divisions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_revenue_divisions_in_Andhra_Pradesh), administered by [Revenue Divisional Officers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sub-divisional_magistrate) which comprise 688 [mandals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_mandals_of_Andhra_Pradesh) administered by [Tehsildars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tehsildar).[^cite_note-Data-232][^cite_note-Brit-233]&#x20;

The local administration consists of a [single tier system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Municipal_governance_in_India) for urban areas, which are governed by either of the three types of urban bodies–[municipal corporation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Municipal_corporation_\(India\)), [municipality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Municipal_council_\(India\)) or [nagar panchayat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nagar_panchayat), based on revenue and population.[^cite_note-Brit-233][^cite_note-LB-234] As of 2026, there are 123 [urban local bodies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_urban_local_bodies_in_Andhra_Pradesh) including 17 municipal corporations, 88 municipalities, and 18 nagar panchayats in the state.[^cite_note-235] The rural administration consists of a three-tier [Panchayati Raj](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Panchayati_raj) system with the [zilla parishad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/District_council_\(India\)) at the district level, [mandal parishad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Panchayat_samiti) at the intermediate (block) level, and [gram panchayats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gram_panchayat) at the village level.[^cite_note-Brit-233][^cite_note-LB-234] As of 2026, there are 26 zilla parishads, 676 mandal parishads, and 13,351 gram panchayats, administered by village administrative officers.[^cite_note-Data-232][^cite_note-236]&#x20;

### Legislature



Secretariat buildings hosting legislative assembly and council, Amaravati

In accordance with the [Constitution of India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitution_of_India), the [governor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Governor_\(India\)) is a state's *de jure* head and appoints the [chief minister](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chief_Minister_\(India\)) who has the *de facto* executive authority.[^cite_note-Brit-233][^cite_note-237] The state has a [bicameral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bicameral) legislature with the [Andhra Pradesh Legislative Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andhra_Pradesh_Legislative_Council), with 58 members, functioning as the upper house.[^cite_note-238] There are 175 [assembly constituencies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_constituencies_of_the_Andhra_Pradesh_Legislative_Assembly) in the state each of which elects a member to the [Andhra Pradesh Legislative Assembly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andhra_Pradesh_Legislative_Assembly), which is the lower house. The state has 11 seats in the [Rajya Sabha](232210.md), the upper house of the [Parliament of India](410238.md), and elects 25 members to the [Lok Sabha](275456.md), the lower house.[^cite_note-239]&#x20;

### Financials

For 2024–25, the government had total receipts of ₹2,693.14 billion (US\$28 billion). The net receipt was ₹1,681.21 billion (US\$17 billion) after accounting for ₹1,101.93 billion (US\$11 billion) of loans. The net expenditure was ₹2,491.34 billion (US\$26 billion), with the [fiscal deficit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fiscal_deficit) at ₹810.13 billion (US\$8.4 billion), which was 5.1% of the state GDP. The revenue consisted of ₹894.35 billion (US\$9.3 billion) of state taxes, ₹597.2 billion (US\$6.2 billion) of non-tax revenue, ₹520.8 billion (US\$5.4 billion) as state's share from central taxes, and ₹201.88 billion (US\$2.1 billion) from central government aid and grants. The allocation to government departments contributed to 61% of the state's expenditure, followed by 24% for salaries and pension. Welfare of backward communities and minorities was allocated the greatest share of ₹361.6 billion (US\$3.8 billion), followed by education, sports, and culture at ₹296.1 billion (US\$3.1 billion).[^cite_note-GDP-3][^cite_note-PRS-240]&#x20;

### Law and order



[High Court of Andhra Pradesh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/High_Court_of_Andhra_Pradesh) at Amaravati

[Andhra Pradesh High Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andhra_Pradesh_High_Court) at Amaravati is the highest judicial authority of the state with control over all the civil and criminal courts in the state. It is headed by a [chief justice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chief_justice) and has a sanctioned strength of 37 judges. The district judiciary is organised in a three-tier system with district courts at the top, civil (senior) and assistant sessions courts in the middle and civil (junior) and judicial first-class magistrate courts at the lower level. Apart from these, there are family courts, special courts, land reforms appellate and industrial tribunals.[^cite_note-241]&#x20;

[Andhra Pradesh Police](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andhra_Pradesh_Police) operates under the home ministry of the state govrenment and is responsible for maintaining law and order in the state.[^cite_note-242] As of 2023, the state had 1021 police stations.[^cite_note-243] As per the [National Crime Records Bureau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Crime_Records_Bureau), 153,867 cases were filed in the state in 2023 translating to a crime rate of 2,891 per million. These included 25,503 cases for crimes against women and children and 2,341 [cyber crime](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cyber_crime) cases.[^cite_note-244][^cite_note-245]&#x20;

### Politics



[Neelam Sanjiva Reddy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neelam_Sanjiva_Reddy), who was the [chief minister](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chief_Minister_of_Andhra_Pradesh) of the state in the 1950s, later became the [President of India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/President_of_India) in 1977

Further information: [Politics of Andhra Pradesh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Politics_of_Andhra_Pradesh)

Since the formation of the state, [Indian National Congress](149333.md) (INC) held a monopoly on governance from 1956 to 1983.[^cite_note-CM-246] Thereafter, the [Telugu Desam Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Telugu_Desam_Party) (TDP), led by [N. T. Rama Rao](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/N._T._Rama_Rao), came to power after the [1983 Andhra Pradesh Legislative Assembly election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1983_Andhra_Pradesh_Legislative_Assembly_election).[^cite_note-247] The INC and TDP alternated to rule the state for the next thirty years.[^cite_note-CM-246] After Telangana was bifurcated in 2013, the TDP formed the first government of the residual state in [2014](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_Andhra_Pradesh_Legislative_Assembly_election), with [Chandrababu Naidu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chandrababu_Naidu) as the chief minister.[^cite_note-248] In the [2019 elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Andhra_Pradesh_Legislative_Assembly_election), the [YSR Congress Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/YSR_Congress_Party), led by [Y. S. Jagan Mohan Reddy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Y._S._Jagan_Mohan_Reddy), came to power, winning 151 out of 175 seats.[^cite_note-249][^cite_note-250] In the [2024 assembly elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Andhra_Pradesh_Legislative_Assembly_election), the TDP-led [National Democratic Alliance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Democratic_Alliance) with [Janasena Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Janasena_Party) and [Bharatiya Janata Party](149330.md) emerged victorious, winning 164 seats, and Naidu became the chief minister of the state for the fourth time.[^cite_note-251]&#x20;

[Sarvepalli Radhakrishnan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sarvepalli_Radhakrishnan), who served as the first [Vice President of India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vice_President_of_India) from 1952 to 1962 and [President of India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/President_of_India) from 1962 to 1967, hailed from [Sarvepalli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sarvepalli) in [Nellore district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nellore_district).[^cite_note-252] [V. V. Giri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/V._V._Giri), who was the president from 1969 to 1974 hailed from a Telugu family from [East Godavari district](674886.md).[^cite_note-253] [Neelam Sanjeeva Reddy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neelam_Sanjeeva_Reddy), who served as the chief minister for two terms in the 1950s and 1960s,[^cite_note-CM-246] later served as the president from 1977 to 1982.[^cite_note-254] [P. V. Narasimha Rao](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/P._V._Narasimha_Rao), who was the chief minister of the state from 1971 to 1973,[^cite_note-CM-246] later served as the [Prime Minister of India](24452.md) from 1991 to 1996.[^cite_note-255]&#x20;

## Economy

Main article: [Economy of Andhra Pradesh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economy_of_Andhra_Pradesh)

The state had an estimated [Gross state domestic product](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gross_state_product) (GSDP) of ₹17.62 trillion (US\$180 billion) for 2025-26 (at current prices), a ten percent increase from 15.91 t (15.66 long tons; 17.54 short tons) in the previous financial year. In 2024-25, [agriculture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agriculture), [manufacturing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manufacturing), and services sectors contributed 37%, 21%, and 42% respectively. The per capita GDP is estimated at ₹298,058 (US\$3,100) for 2025-26.[^cite_note-GDP-3] According to the *Economic Survey of India 2024–25*, the state was identified as the "leading performer" in the agriculture and allied sectors, with an annual growth rate of 8.8%.[^cite_note-256]&#x20;

As per a [NITI Aayog](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NITI_Aayog) report, the [poverty rate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poverty_rate) was recorded as 4.2% in 2023, down from 11.77% in 2015–16.[^cite_note-257] As per the labour force survey for the period of July 2022 to June 2023, the state had an [unemployment rate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unemployment_rate) of 24%, the third highest in the country, with the national rate at 13.4%.[^cite_note-258]&#x20;

### Agriculture



[Rice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rice) is the staple crop in the state; Pictured paddy fields in [Konaseema district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Konaseema_district)

The agricultural economy of the state comprises of agriculture and agro industries, [livestock](25160767.md) and [poultry](23197.md) farming, and [fisheries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fisheries).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDES202131-120] The state had a total cultivable area is 19.904 million acres in 2024.[^cite_note-Irig-259] In 2020-21, about 62.2% of the able population was engaged in agriculture and related activities.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDES2021vii_(Executive_summary)-260] [Rice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rice) is the major food crop and staple food,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDES20219-261] while other crops include [jowar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sorghum_bicolor), [bajra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pearl_millet), [maize](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maize), [pulses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legume), [sugarcane](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sugarcane), [cotton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cotton), [tobacco](30942.md), [fruits](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fruits), and [vegetables](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vegetables).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDES2021110-113-262] The state contributed to 30% of the total fish production of India and it had a share of 35% in total sea food exports of India in 2022–23.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDES20216-263]&#x20;

The state has three designated agricultural export zones: Chittoor region for mango pulp and vegetables, Krishna region for mangoes, and Guntur region for [chillies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chili_pepper).[^cite_note-264] [Banaganapalle mangoes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Banganapalle_\(mango\)) produced in the state were accorded a [Geographical Indication](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geographical_Indication) status in 2017.[^cite_note-GIlist-161][^cite_note-265] Government run Rythu Seva Kendras serve as farmer facilitation centres for disbursing agricultural requirements like seeds and manure, for the procurement of harvests, and for financial services.[^cite_note-266][^cite_note-267] As of 2023, the government operated 10,778 such centres in the state.[^cite_note-268]&#x20;

In 2014, the Commission on Inclusive and Sustainable Agricultural Development of Andhra Pradesh estimated about 2.43 million farmers in the state of which 0.63 million were landless tenants, who cultivated about 44% of the total cultivated land. As per the provisions of the Crop Cultivator Rights Act (CCRA) of 2019, an effort was made to identify such farmers. However, by 2022, only 26% of the estimated had obtained or loan eligibility cards.[^cite_note-269] While the state government enacted the Land Titling Act in 2023 to modify the presumptive land ownership system based on possession, registration or inheritance documents to conclusive land ownership system,[^cite_note-270] it was repealed in 2024 due to the scope for misuse of authority and deviation from the central government's draft bill.[^cite_note-271]&#x20;

### Industries



Rocket launch at the [Satish Dhawan Space Centre](2496664.md), Sriharikota

As per the annual survey of industries 2019–20, the state had 12,582 factories which employed 681,224 people. [Food products](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Food_products) (25.48%), [minerals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minerals) (11.26%), [textiles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Textiles) (9.35%), and [pharmaceuticals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pharmaceuticals) (8.68%) were the major contributing sectors in terms of the number of factories. In terms of the total Gross value of ₹550.35 billion (US\$5.7 billion), food products (18.95%), pharmaceuticals (17.01%), and non-metallic minerals (16.25%) were the top three contributors. The top contributing districts were [Visakhapatnam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Visakhapatnam_district), [Tirupati](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tirupati_district), and [Krishna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Krishna_district).[^cite_note-272]&#x20;

The defence administered [Hindustan Shipyard Limited](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hindustan_Shipyard_Limited) at Visakhapatnam is a major ship building yard and built the first ship in India in 1948.[^cite_note-273] The mining sector contributed ₹33.9 billion (US\$350 million) in revenue in 2021–22.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDOP20239-274] About 225 million barrels of crude oil were produced from the [Ravva oil field](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ravva_oil_field), located in the offshore area of the Krishna–Godavari basin during 1994–2011.[^cite_note-275] The state accounted for 0.6% of codensate production, and 2.9% of natural gas production of India in 2020–21.[^cite_note-PNG-116] There are 36 automotive manufacturing facilities in the state.[^cite_note-276]&#x20;



[Sri City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sri_City), a [Special Economic Zone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special_Economic_Zone)

Andhra Pradesh has over 50 notified [Special Economic Zones](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special_Economic_Zone) across various sectors including [information technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Information_technology), pharmaceuticals, [biotechnology](4502.md), and textiles. Major such zones include [Visakhapatnam Special Economic Zone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Visakhapatnam_Special_Economic_Zone), [Sri City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sri_City), and [Andhra Pradesh Special Economic Zone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andhra_Pradesh_Special_Economic_Zone).[^cite_note-277][^cite_note-278] As of 20 June 2023 there were 190 science and technology organisations in the state, including 12 central labs and research institutions.[^cite_note-279] The [Satish Dhawan Space Centre](2496664.md), operated by the [Indian Space Research Organisation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Space_Research_Organisation), is located on the barrier island of [Sriharikota](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sriharikota) and is India's primary rocket launching station.[^cite_note-280]&#x20;

### Services

Trade, hotels and restaurants, public administration, and information technology formed the major services category.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDOP202329-281] In 2021-22, the [information technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Information_technology_in_India) exports from the state was valued at ₹9.26 billion (US\$96 million), about 0.14% of the IT exports from India.[^cite_note-282] The state is ranked third in domestic tourist footfalls for the year 2021, with 93.2 million domestic tourists, amounting to 13.8% of all domestic tourists in India. A major share of the tourists visited Visakhapatnam and temples in Tirupati, Vijayawada, and Srisailam.[^cite_note-283]&#x20;

## Transportation

Main article: [Transport in Andhra Pradesh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transport_in_Andhra_Pradesh)

### Roads



An intracity bus operated by the [Andhra Pradesh State Road Transport Corporation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andhra_Pradesh_State_Road_Transport_Corporation)&#x20;

See also: [List of state highways in Andhra Pradesh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_state_highways_in_Andhra_Pradesh)

As of 2023 the state had a road network of 53,484.72 km (33,233.86 mi) comprising of 8,163.72 km (5,072.70 mi) of [national highways](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_highways_of_India), 12,595.60 km (7,826.54 mi) of [state highways](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_highways_in_India), 26,485.51 km (16,457.33 mi) of major district roads, and 6,239.89 km (3,877.29 mi) of other roads. These include 3,898.19 km (2,422.22 mi) of four or six-lane roads and expressways.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDOP2023168-284] There are 52 national highways including the [National Highway 16](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Highway_16_\(India\)), with forms part of the [Golden Quadrilateral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golden_Quadrilateral) network.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDOP2023170-285][^cite_note-286]&#x20;

The state government-owned [Andhra Pradesh State Road Transport Corporation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andhra_Pradesh_State_Road_Transport_Corporation) provides public bus services, It operates a fleet of 11,098 buses across 129 depots, ferrying 3.68 million passengers daily.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDOP2023176-287] As of 2023, the state had 1.83 million transport vehicles, of which goods carriers constituted 53.6%, and [auto rickshaws](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Auto_rickshaws) 36.2%. There were 13.7 million non-transport vehicles, of which [motorcycles](19876.md) constituted 89.5%, and four-wheelers constituted 7.3%.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDOP2023173-288]&#x20;

### Rail



[Vijayawada Junction railway station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vijayawada_Junction_railway_station)

In 1843, [Arthur Cotton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arthur_Cotton) constructed a short railway line along the beach in Visakhapatnam. In 1845, a railway line was established for ferrying and construction materials for building a dam near [Rajamahendravaram](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rajamahendravaram). The [Madras Railway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madras_Railway) established a rail connection from [Madras](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royapuram_railway_station) to [Renigunta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Renigunta_Junction_railway_station) in September 1862.[^cite_note-289] The Rajahmundry–Visakhapatnam line was established in 1893 and the link between Madras and [Vijayawada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vijayawada_Junction_railway_station) was established in 1899, thereby completing the [Howrah-Chennai main line](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Howrah-Chennai_main_line).[^cite_note-290][^cite_note-291]&#x20;

As of 2022, the state has a total [broad-gauge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Broad-gauge_railway) railway length of 3,969 km (2,466 mi).[^cite_note-292][^cite_note-293] The railway network forms part of the [South Central railway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Central_Railway_zone),[^cite_note-294] [East Coast railway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_Coast_Railway_zone),[^cite_note-295] [South Western railway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Western_Railway_zone),[^cite_note-296] and [Southern Railway zones](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Railway_zone).[^cite_note-297] [South Coast Railway zone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Coast_Railway_zone), with headquarters in Visakhapatnam, was announced in 2019,[^cite_note-298][^cite_note-299] and came to effect on 1 June 2026.[^cite_note-300] There are 180+ railway stations in the state,[^cite_note-301] including 26 major stations.[^cite_note-302] [Visakhapatnam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Visakhapatnam_railway_station) was declared [the cleanest railway station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_cleanest_railway_stations_in_India) in the country, as per an assessment in 2018.[^cite_note-303] [Shimiliguda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shimiliguda_railway_station) is one of the highest altitude broad-gauge stations in the country.[^cite_note-304]&#x20;

### Air

[Map](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special\:Map/5/16/80.5/en)

Airports in Andhra Pradesh

\
&#x20;

Further information: [List of airports in Andhra Pradesh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_airports_in_Andhra_Pradesh)

[Tirupati Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tirupati_Airport) was established in 1976.[^cite_note-305] Flight services started at [Visakhapatnam Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/INS_Dega) in the same year, before it was transferred to the [Indian Navy](604522.md) in 1986, and was later established as a [civil enclave](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joint-use_airport) alongside the airbase *[INS Dega](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/INS_Dega)*.[^cite_note-306][^cite_note-307] [Vijayawada Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vijayawada_Airport) began commercial operations in 2003.[^cite_note-308] Apart from these three airports, which are authorised to cater to international flights, the state has [domestic airports](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Domestic_airport) at [Rajamahendravaram](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rajahmundry_Airport), [Kadapa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kadapa_Airport), and [Kurnool](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kurnool_Airport).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDOP2023180-183-309] A privately owned airport is located [Puttaparthi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sri_Sathya_Sai_Airport).[^cite_note-310] The [Alluri Sitarama Raju International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alluri_Sitarama_Raju_International_Airport) was opened for commercial operations on 17 August 2026.[^cite_note-311][^cite_note-312] In 2021-22, the state catered to a domestic air passenger traffic of 2.47 million.[^cite_note-313]&#x20;

### Water

Further information: [List of ports and harbours of Andhra Pradesh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_ports_and_harbours_of_Andhra_Pradesh)

The state has one major port at [Visakhapatnam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Visakhapatnam_Port) under the administrative control of the central government and 15 notified ports, including three captive ports, under the control of the state government.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDOP2023183-314] Visakhapatnam port was commissioned in 1933.[^cite_note-315] [Krishnapatnam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Krishnapatnam_Port), [Gangavaram](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gangavaram_Port), and [Kakinada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kakinada_Port) are the other major seaports.[^cite_note-316] About 189.21 million tonnes of cargo was handled by the five largest ports in 2023-24.[^cite_note-317] As of 2023, new sea ports were under construction at [Machilipatnam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Machilipatnam_Port), [Mulapeta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mulapeta_Port), and [Ramayapatnam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ramayapatnam_Port).[^cite_note-318]&#x20;

## Infrastructure

### Water supply



[Polavaram Project](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polavaram_Project)

The state has 40 major and medium rivers including the Godavari, Krishna, and Penna rivers and 40,000 minor streams and irrigation canals, which irrigate 10.172 million acres of land.[^cite_note-Irig-259] Following the split of Telangana in 2014, there have been [disputes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Disputes_between_Andhra_Pradesh_and_Telangana) regarding the sharing of Krishna and Godavari River waters between the states.[^cite_note-319]&#x20;

The [Dowleswaram Barrage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dowleswaram_Barrage) is an irrigation structure built on the Godavari River in the mid 19th century.[^cite_note-320] The [Nagarjuna Sagar Dam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nagarjuna_Sagar_Dam) was commissioned in 1967 and the [Srisailam Dam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Srisailam_Dam) was commissioned in 1982.[^cite_note-321][^cite_note-322] [Veligonda Project](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Veligonda_Project), serving the needs of united [Prakasam](3360237.md), [Nellore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nellore_district), and [Kadapa districts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kadapa_district), was inaugurated in August 2026.[^cite_note-323]&#x20;

[Polavaram Project](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polavaram_Project) is an under construction multi purpose project located 42 km (26 mi) upstream of Dowleshwaram, with a planned reservoir capacity of 194.6 [Tmcft](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tmcft).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDOP2023136-324][^cite_note-325][^cite_note-326] Annamayya project is a planned irrigation project in the Annamayya district.[^cite_note-327]&#x20;

### Power and energy



[Rayalaseema Thermal Power Station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rayalaseema_Thermal_Power_Station)

Further information: [Power sector of Andhra Pradesh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Power_sector_of_Andhra_Pradesh)

As of October 2023, the state has an installed power capacity of 28,317 MW with 9,973 MW originating from the public sector and 18,354 MW from the private sector. [Thermal power](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thermal_power) was the largest contributor with 17,325 MW, and [Renewable energy](25784.md) contributed to the remaining 10,992 MW.[^cite_note-PW-328][^cite_note-329] As of 2022, the annual energy consumption was 68,972 million [Kwh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kilowatt-hour) with a per capita consumption was 1,285 Kwh and a peak power demand of 2,032 MW.[^cite_note-PD-330] The energy consumption increased to 69,113 million Kwh in 2023–42.[^cite_note-331]&#x20;

### Communication

As of 2023, the state had 10,605 post offices.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDOP2023189-332] State owned [Bharat Sanchar Nigam Limited](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bharat_Sanchar_Nigam_Limited) (BSNL) operated 1,310 [fixed line](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fixed_line) telephone exchanges with 20.6 million landline connections.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDOP2023186-333] BSNL and private operators including [Bharti Airtel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bharti_Airtel), [Reliance Jio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reliance_Jio), and [Vodafone Idea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vodafone_Idea) provided [mobile](19644137.md) services in the state.[^cite_note-TRAI-334] Mobile network services are available in 15,322 out of 17,328 villages, including [5G](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/5G) services provided in select localities. There were 82 million mobile phone users including 67.1 million [internet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Internet) subscribers.[^cite_note-335] [Broadband](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Broadband) and [Internet Protocol television](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Internet_Protocol_television) services were provided by the state owned [Andhra Pradesh State FiberNet Limited](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andhra_Pradesh_State_FiberNet_Limited) and other private oparators.[^cite_note-TRAI-334][^cite_note-336] BSNL operates a [Wide Area Network](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wide_Area_Network) connecting 2,164 government offices across 668 locations,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDOP2023204-337] including dedicated links between the district headquarters and the state capital.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDOP2023195-338]&#x20;

### Healthcare



[King George Hospital, Visakhapatnam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/King_George_Hospital,_Visakhapatnam)

As of 2019, there were 13 district hospitals, 28 sub-district hospitals, and 6,049 urban and rural health centres in the state. The government employed 42,060 Accredited Social Health Activists (ASHA) to provide doorstep healthcare support services.[^cite_note-339] The [108](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/108_\(emergency_telephone_number\)) service provides emergency services and the 104 service provides health care services at the doorstep of villages through mobile medical units.[^cite_note-340]&#x20;

The central and state governments provides free [health insurance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Health_insurance) up to a limit of ₹2.5 million (US\$26,000) to the poor families.[^cite_note-341] Patients under the state insurance scheme are treated in government hospitals, and 2,700 private hospitals including 540 tertiary care hospitals. About 1.39 million patients were treated at a cost of ₹33.5 billion (US\$350 million) in 2023–24.[^cite_note-342] A 2022 study indicated that the [mortality rate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mortality_rate) has decreased amongst the beneficiaries with a lower average claim amount adjusted for [inflation](38286.md), indicating better outcomes at a lower cost.[^cite_note-343] The National family health survey conducted between 2019 to 2021 indicated that at least 70% of the households had at least one member covered under the government insurance schemes.[^cite_note-344]&#x20;

### Banking

As of March 2023, there were 7,881 branches operated by 33 private sector banks, 12 public sector banks, three rural banks, three small finance banks, three payment banks, one cooperative bank, and one state financial corporation operating in the state.[^cite_note-345] As of September 2022 the banks held [fixed deposits](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fixed_deposits) amounting to ₹4.35 trillion (US\$45 billion) and had extended credit amounting to ₹6.28 trillion (US\$65 billion), with a credit to deposit ratio of 1.44, which is higher than the norm of 0.6 set by the [Reserve Bank of India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reserve_Bank_of_India). Loans to the primary sector including agriculture amounted to 60.1% of the total credit.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDOP2023186-333] In the early 2010s, Andhra Pradesh contributed to nearly two-thirds of the national share of [microfinance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Microfinance) loans. However, due to newly enacted regulatory norms, there has been a drop in such loans.[^cite_note-346]&#x20;

## Education



A [zilla parishad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zilla_parishad) high school

Further information: [Education in Andhra Pradesh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Education_in_Andhra_Pradesh) and [List of institutions of higher education in Andhra Pradesh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_institutions_of_higher_education_in_Andhra_Pradesh)

Primary and secondary school education is imparted by government and private schools, regulated by the school education department of the state government.[^cite_note-347][^cite_note-348] The state follows the [10+2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/10%2B2) system of schooling, and in 2020, the state government classified the schools into six categories–satellite foundation schools (pre-primary), foundational schools (pre-primary – class II), foundational school plus (pre-primary – class V), pre-high school (class III – VII/VIII), high school (class III – X), and high school plus (class III – XII).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDOP202312-349] In 2021, government schools which earlier used Telugu as the medium of instruction,[^cite_note-350] were transitioned to English-medium, and 1,000 of them were affiliated to the [Central Board of Secondary Education](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Board_of_Secondary_Education) as a part of an educational revamp programme, partly funded by the [World Bank](36752.md).[^cite_note-351][^cite_note-352] Private schools aided by the government were converted to either government or private schools during the transition.[^cite_note-353] There were 8.25 million students enrolled in 61,948 schools across the state in 2021.[^cite_note-354] In March 2024, 616,615 students appeared for the [Class X](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Secondary_School_Leaving_Certificate) exams,[^cite_note-355][^cite_note-356] and 393,757 students appeared for the [higher secondary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Higher_secondary) exams.[^cite_note-357][^cite_note-358]&#x20;



[Andhra University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andhra_University), the oldest university in the state

The state council of higher education organises various entrance tests for different streams and conducts counselling for college admissions.[^cite_note-359] The Andhra Pradesh State Skill Development Corporation is responsible for technical skill development and placement for the educated.[^cite_note-360] The gross enrolment ratio (GER) in higher education for the age group 18–23 was at 35.2% in 2019–20, higher than the national GER of 27.1%.[^cite_note-UGC1-361]<sup>: 93 </sup> There are 36 universities including three central universities, 23 public universities, six private universities, and four deemed universities.[^cite_note-UGC1-361]<sup>: 100 </sup> [Andhra University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andhra_University), established in 1926, is the oldest university in the state.[^cite_note-362][^cite_note-363] [Dr. NTR University of Health Sciences](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dr._NTR_University_of_Health_Sciences) is responsible for overseeing medical education in the 348 affiliated colleges in the state.[^cite_note-364] As of 2023, there were 685 institutions offering diploma, undergraduate, and postgraduate courses, with an annual intake of 299,608 students.[^cite_note-365] These included 169 government and 55 government-aided colleges. There were 85 government and 175 private polytechnic colleges with a sanctioned strength of 75,906 students.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDOP202314-366] In 2020, there were 510 industrial training institutes including 82 run by the government, which had 93,280 seats.[^cite_note-367]&#x20;

As of 2021, the state has 2,510 public libraries, including four regional libraries and 13 district central libraries under government management.[^cite_note-368] There are few privately-run libraries including the [Saraswata Niketanam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saraswata_Niketanam), one of the oldest libraries in the state, which was established in 1918.[^cite_note-369] In the early 2020s, the state government announced plans to develop digital libraries in rural areas.[^cite_note-370]&#x20;

## Media

In 1902, *[Krishna Patrika](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Krishna_Patrika)* was the first Telugu daily published from the region, and the *[Andhra Patrika](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andhra_Patrika)* was first published in 1908.[^cite_note-371] As of September 2020, there were 5,798 registered newspapers and periodicals in the state, including 1,645 dailies, 817 weeklies, 2,431 monthlies, and 623 fortnightlies. There were 787 Telugu dailies with an average circulation of 9.9 million and 103 English dailies with an average circulation of 1.6 million.[^cite_note-372][^cite_note-373] *[Eenadu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eenadu)*, *[Sakshi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sakshi_\(newspaper\))*, and *[Andhra Jyothi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andhra_Jyothi)* were the top three Telugu dailies in terms of circulation and the top three Telugu news sites by number of visits.[^cite_note-374][^cite_note-375]&#x20;

There are more than 50 private satellite television channels,[^cite_note-376][^cite_note-377] with the most viewed channels being [Star Maa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Star_Maa), [Zee Telugu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zee_Telugu), [ETV Telugu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ETV_Telugu), and [Sun Gemini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sun_Gemini).[^cite_note-378] [BBC](19344654.md) launched a Telugu news channel on 2 October 2017.[^cite_note-379][^cite_note-380] As of 2019, [All India Radio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/All_India_Radio) operated several radio stations, which were broadcast from 14 locations in the state.[^cite_note-381] As of 2022, four private operators–[Red FM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red_FM_93.5), [Radio Mirchi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Radio_Mirchi), [E FM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eenadu), and [Radio City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Radio_City_\(Indian_radio_station\)), ran 13 [FM radio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FM_radio) stations across five cities.[^cite_note-382] Privately held media organisations often exhibit editorial leanings aligned with specific political parties operating within the state.[^cite_note-383][^cite_note-384]&#x20;

## Sports

Main article: [Sports in Andhra Pradesh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sports_in_Andhra_Pradesh)



[ACA–VDCA Cricket Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ACA%E2%80%93VDCA_Cricket_Stadium) in [Visakhapatnam](238760.md)

[Kabaddi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kabaddi) is a [contact sport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Contact_sport) which is the state game of Andhra Pradesh.[^cite_note-385] [Traditional games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Traditional_games_of_Andhra_Pradesh) include [Dagudu Mootalu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hide-and-seek), [Tokkudu Billa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tokkudu_Billa), [Yedu Penkulata](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seven_stones), [Vamanaguntalu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pallanguzhi), [Chadarangam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chaturanga), [Puli Joodam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Puli_Joodam), [Ashta Chamma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ashta_Chamma_%E2%80%93_Board_Game), [Vaikuntapali](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Snakes_and_ladders), [Nalugu Stambalata](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nalugu_Stambhalata_\(game\)), and [Nalugu Ralla Aata](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nalugu_Rallu_Aata_%E2%80%93_Game_of_4_stones).[^cite_note-386] Karra Samu is a traditional [martial art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martial_art) form practised in the state.[^cite_note-387] [Cricket](25675557.md) is a popular sport.[^cite_note-388] The state has two international cricket stadiums–[ACA–VDCA Cricket Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ACA%E2%80%93VDCA_Cricket_Stadium) in Visakhapatnam,[^cite_note-389] and [ACA International Cricket Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ACA_International_Cricket_Stadium) in Vijayawada.[^cite_note-390]&#x20;

Sports Authority of Andhra Pradesh is the governmental body that is responsible for sports infrastructure development, coaching, and administration of sporting programmes.[^cite_note-391] [Sports Authority of India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sports_Authority_of_India) operates three training centres in the state.[^cite_note-392] Athletes from the state won eleven medals in the [2022 Asian games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Asian_games).[^cite_note-393] The state won 16 medals at the 36th National Games held in 2022 and was ranked 21st in the overall medal table.[^cite_note-394] The state team was ranked at 13th spot in the sixth edition of the [Khelo India Youth Games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khelo_India_Youth_Games) in 2023 with 27 medals.[^cite_note-395]&#x20;

Notable sportspeople from the state include [Karnam Malleswari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karnam_Malleswari), the first Indian woman to win an [Olympic medal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olympic_medal) at the [2000 Summer Olympics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2000_Summer_Olympics).[^cite_note-396] [Pullela Gopichand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pullela_Gopichand), the second Indian to win the [All England Open Badminton Championships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/All_England_Open_Badminton_Championships) in 2001,[^cite_note-397] [P. V. Sindhu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/P._V._Sindhu), the first Indian to win the [BWF World Championships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BWF_World_Championships) in 2019 and winner of two individual medals in the [Summer Olympics](27566.md),[^cite_note-398] and [Srikanth Kidambi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Srikanth_Kidambi), the first Indian male to win a silver medal in the BWF World Championships.[^cite_note-399]&#x20;
